<p><span class="pn">03</span> To provide additional emphasis, large surfaces such as bridge piers may be painted with diagonal stripes, 12 inches or greater in width, similar in design to the Type 3 object marker.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The alternating black and retroreflective yellow stripes (OM3-L, OM3-R) shall be sloped down at an angle of 45 degrees toward the side on which traffic is to pass the obstruction. If traffic can pass to either side of the obstruction, the alternating black and retroreflective yellow stripes (OM3-C) shall form chevrons that point upwards.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04a</span> <span class="ca">The OM3-L or OM3-R object marker should be in line with the inner edge of the obstruction.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Appropriate signs (see Sections 2B.40 and 2C.23) directing traffic to one or both sides of the obstruction may be used instead of the object marker.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> <span class="ca">If any object marker is located behind the guard rail, all of the marker panel should be visible to approaching traffic. </span></p>
</div>

<span id="2c72" class="anchor"></span>

## 2C.72 Object Markers for Obstructions Adjacent to the Roadway

<div class="secmeta">Section 2C.72 &middot; manual page 344 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2C.72/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Obstructions not actually within the roadway are sometimes so close to the edge of the road that they need a marker. These include underpass piers, bridge abutments, handrails, ends of traffic barriers, utility poles, and culvert headwalls. In other cases there might not be a physical object involved, but other roadside conditions exist, such as narrow shoulders, drop-offs, gores, small islands, and abrupt changes in the roadway alignment, that might make it undesirable for a road user to leave the roadway, and therefore would create a need for a marker.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Type 2 or Type 3 object markers may be used to mark an obstruction adjacent to the roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> If a Type 2 or Type 3 object marker is used to mark an obstruction adjacent to the roadway, the edge of the object marker that is closest to the road user should be installed in line with the closest edge of the obstruction.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> When a marker is applied to the approach ends of guardrail or crash cushion terminals it should have the appearance of a Type 3 object marker and should be directly affixed, without a substrate, to the approach end of the guardrail or crash cushion and generally conform to the size and shape of the approach end of the guardrail or crash cushion.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Type 1 and Type 4 object markers shall not be used to mark obstructions adjacent to the roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Standard warning signs in this Chapter should also be used where applicable.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> <span class="ca">Objects in a paved area within 8 feet of the traveled way may be marked with a Type 3 object marker. Objects outside of the </span> <span class="ca">paved shoulder, within 12 feet of the traveled way, may be marked with Type 2 object markers. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> <span class="ca">Type 2 object markers may be placed in front of, alongside of, or attached to the object. Where objects are very close to each </span> <span class="ca">other, only the first object may need to be marked. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> <span class="ca">The Type L(CA) Utility Pole marker may be used to mark a utility pole. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> <span class="ca">If used, the utility company should be responsible for installing and maintaining the Type L(CA) Utility Pole marker. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c10">Section 2C.10</a> and 2C.43 for use of Type 1 (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/om1-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/OM1-3.svg">OM1-3</a>) object markers in conjunction with One-Directional Large Arrow </span> <span class="ca">(<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W1-6.svg">W1-6</a>) and Two-Direction Large Arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w1-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W1-7.svg">W1-7</a>) signs for abrupt changes in the roadway alignment. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/6h/#6h104ca">Section 6H.104(CA)</a> for use of Type 1 and Type 3 object markers for temporary traffic control. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> <span class="ca">If engineering judgment indicates that the exit gore at an interchange cannot be negotiated in a reasonable manner, then in </span> <span class="ca">addition to the Type F and G delineators, a OM3-C object marker may be used as shown in <a href="#fig-3g-102ca">Figure 3G-102(CA)</a>. </span></p>
</div>

<span id="2c73" class="anchor"></span>

## 2C.73 Object Markers for Ends of Roadways

<div class="secmeta">Section 2C.73 &middot; manual page 345 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2C.73/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The Type 4 object marker is used to warn and alert road users of the end of a roadway in other than construction or maintenance areas.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If an object marker is used to mark the end of a roadway, a Type 4 object marker shall be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The Type 4 object marker may be used in instances where there are no alternate vehicular paths.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03a</span> <span class="ca">The Type 4 object marker shall be used at the end of a road or cul-de-sac street where there is no alternate vehicular </span> <span class="ca">path. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Where conditions warrant, more than one marker, or a larger marker with or without a Type 3 Barricade (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b75">Section 2B.75</a>), may be used at the end of the roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">04a</span> <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c24">Section 2C.24</a> for use of end-of-roadway marker in conjunction with END (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w31ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/w31ca.webp">W31(CA)</a>) sign. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The minimum mounting height, measured vertically from the bottom of a Type 4 object marker to the elevation of the near edge of the traveled way, shall be 4 feet.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Appropriate advance warning signs in this Chapter should be used.</p>
</div>

<span id="2c101ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2C.101(CA) TRACTOR-SEMIS OVER \_\_\_ FEET KINGPIN TO REAR AXLE NOT ADVISED (SW48(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2C.101(CA) &middot; manual page 345 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2C.101(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> <span class="ca">The TRACTOR-SEMIS OVER ___ FEET KINGPIN TO REAR AXLE NOT ADVISED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw48ca.webp">SW48(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2c-6ca">Figure 2C-6(CA)</a>) </span> <span class="ca">may be used on certain specified conventional highways and freeways that have restricted turning radii. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <span class="ca">At freeway off-ramps to restricted conventional highways, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw48ca.webp">SW48(CA)</a> sign shall be installed with a NEXT EXIT </span> <span class="ca">(<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw48-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw48-1pca.webp">SW48-1P(CA)</a>) plaque. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> <span class="ca">The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw48ca.webp">SW48(CA)</a> sign should be located far enough in advance of the restricted area to allow the vehicle operator time to select </span> <span class="ca">an alternate route. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> <span class="ca">The NEXT EXIT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw48-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw48-1pca.webp">SW48-1P(CA)</a>) plaque (refer to <a href="#fig-2c-6ca">Figure 2C-6(CA)</a>) or Distance plaque may supplement the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw48ca.webp">SW48(CA)</a> sign, as </span> <span class="ca">appropriate. Alternate messages for the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw48-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw48-1pca.webp">SW48-1P(CA)</a> plaque may be NEXT RIGHT, SECOND EXIT, SECOND RIGHT, NEXT LEFT </span> <span class="ca">or SECOND LEFT. </span></p>
</div>

<span id="2c102ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2C.102(CA) Weight Limit signs (W20(CA) and W20A(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2C.102(CA) &middot; manual page 345 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2C.102(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> <span class="ca">The Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w20ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/w20ca.webp">W20(CA)</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w20aca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/w20aca.webp">W20A(CA)</a>) signs (refer to <a href="#fig-2c-6ca">Figure 2C-6(CA)</a>) may be used in combination with a Distance </span> <span class="ca">Plaque, far enough in advance to allow the vehicle operator to select an alternate route.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <span class="ca">An alternative route should be evaluated for height, weight and size restrictions. Appropriate signs should be posted along the </span> <span class="ca">route to advise motorists of any restrictions. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b64">Section 2B.64</a> for regulatory Weight Limit signs.</span></p>
</div>

<span id="2c103ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2C.103(CA) Safety Corridor Sign (SW33(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2C.103(CA) &middot; manual page 346 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2C.103(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> <span class="ca">The Safety Corridor (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw33ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw33ca.webp">SW33(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2c-6ca">Figure 2C-6(CA)</a>) may be installed at the written request of an official Corridor </span> <span class="ca">Safety Task Force on any roadway segment that is designated as a Safety Awareness Zone under the authority of SHS § 97.1. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <span class="ca">More information on Highway Safety Corridors and Task Forces is available at: </span> <span class="ca">http://www.chp.ca.gov/community/corridor.html</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> <span class="ca">The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw33ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw33ca.webp">SW33(CA)</a> sign shall not be installed on freeways.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> <span class="ca">When used, one <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw33ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw33ca.webp">SW33(CA)</a> sign should be posted at each end of the corridor. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw33ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw33ca.webp">SW33(CA)</a> sign specifications should be as </span> <span class="ca">follows: Yellow background with black text having a primary safety message. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> <span class="ca">A secondary message shall be agreed upon by the Task Force. The secondary message shall not cover more than 25 </span> <span class="ca">percent of the sign’s surface area. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> <span class="ca">Caltrans shall purchase and install the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sw33ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sw33ca.webp">SW33(CA)</a> sign. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> <span class="ca">The Task Force is to advise Caltrans, in writing, as to how long the signs are to remain on the highway, but this time period </span> <span class="ca">should not exceed three years. </span></p>
</div>
